巴黎那一周的星期四，是巴黎市的正式接待。到这时，这个小伙子的人气已如日中天。据说有五十万人夹道而立，观看这位飞行员在东道主大使的陪同下驱车而过。

===Original===
At the City Hall, Lindbergh received the Gold Medal of the Municipality of Paris. In a brief speech he told the Council that he believed his flight was the forerunner of a regular commercial air service between the United States and France. He added that Nungesser and Coli would have voiced the same thought if they had landed in America.

===Chinese===
在市政厅，林德伯格接受了巴黎市金质奖章。他在简短的演讲中告诉市议会议员们，他相信自己的飞行是美法之间定期商业空运的先声。他补充说，倘若农热塞尔与科利降落在了美国，他们也会说出同样的想法。

===Original===
Ambassador Herrick then made one of his finest and most widely quoted speeches. "I am not a religious man," he said, "but I believe there are certain things that happen in life which can only be described as the interpretation of a Divine Act. I would not be surprised if this flight marks the beginning of a return of that sympathy and affection which lasted 150 years between France and America. Lindbergh brought you the spirit of America in a manner in which it could never be brought in a diplomatic sack."

===Chinese===
接着，赫里克大使发表了他最精彩、被引用最广的演讲之一。"我不是个虔诚的教徒，"他说，"但我相信，人生中有些事情只能以上苍之意的解读来描述。如果这次飞行标志着法美之间延续了 150 年的那份同情与情谊开始回归，我不会感到意外。林德伯格以任何外交邮袋都无法装运的方式，把美国的精髓带到了你们面前。"

===Original===
Next morning Lindbergh got up at daybreak and went to Le Bourget where he found a small black Nieuport 300 hp fighting plane awaiting him. To the delight of the French fliers as well as the populace he went aloft and began stunting with a skill and ease that stamped him once and for all an expert. Again he rose a peg in French esteem. Nor was this a studied performance any more than his modesty in bearing or his brevity in oratory were studied. It was only another integral part of the "phenomenon of Lindbergh."

===Chinese===
次日清晨，林德伯格天一亮就起床，前往勒布尔热，发现一架黑色小纽波特（Nieuport）300 马力战斗机正等候着他。令法国飞行员和民众同样欣喜的是，他升空开始特技飞行，那份技艺与从容，一锤定音地证明了他是个行家。他在法国人心中的分量又添了一分。这不是一场刻意表演，正如他举止的谦逊、演讲的简短都非刻意一样。它只是"林德伯格现象"又一个不可分割的部分。

===Original===
At noon there was a luncheon at the Ministry of War. Later he was received by the Senators at the Luxembourg Palace. A reception and official visits followed. In the evening he attended a gala performance at the Champs-Élysées Theatre.

===Chinese===
中午，陆军部设午宴。随后他在卢森堡宫（Luxembourg Palace）受到参议员们的接见。接着是招待会和一连串正式拜会。晚上，他出席了香榭丽舍剧院（Champs-Élysées Theatre）的庆典演出。

===Original===
The very recital of his festivities and honors grows monotonous.

===Chinese===
光是罗列他的庆典与荣衔，就已令人生腻。

===Original===
Next day he left. About eight in the morning he motored to Le Bourget and put in three hours grooming his plane for its next flight. At noon he hopped off for Brussels, circling the Eiffel Tower and dropping a note of goodbye and thanks to Paris in the Place de la Concorde on his way.

===Chinese===
第二天他启程了。早晨八点左右，他乘车前往勒布尔热，花了三个小时为飞机的下一段航程整备。中午，他起飞前往布鲁塞尔，途中绕埃菲尔铁塔盘旋一周，在协和广场上空投下一封向巴黎道别与致谢的短信。

## Publisher's Note / 出版者说明

===Original===
When Lindbergh came to tell the story of his welcome at Paris, London, Brussels, Washington, New York and St. Louis he found himself up against a tougher problem than flying the Atlantic.

He wanted to speak from his heart his appreciation for all the kindness and enthusiasm that had been shown him. But when he began to write he found that fitting words would not come. Somehow it wasn't a story for him to tell.

So the publishers agreed to his suggestion that this part of the record be put in the third person by a writer he might choose. As a result much in the way of illuminating speeches and other important matter is included that Lindbergh would have been loath to use.

===Chinese===
当林德伯格要讲述自己在巴黎、伦敦、布鲁塞尔、华盛顿、纽约和圣路易斯受到欢迎的经历时，他发现自己面临着一个比飞越大西洋更棘手的难题。

他想发自内心地表达对人们向他展现的一切善意与热情的感激。但当他提笔写作时，却发现贴切的言辞迟迟不来。不知怎的，这并不是一个适合由他本人来讲述的故事。

于是出版者接受了他的建议：这部分记录由他自选的一位作家以第三人称写成。其结果是，书中收入了许多富有启发性的演说及其他重要材料，这些本是林德伯格本人不愿采用的内容。

## VI Receiving a Pilot's Wings / 六 佩戴飞行员翼章

===Original===
In September, 1924, we were transferred to Kelly. The time we had looked forward to for half a year had arrived. We were through the period of just learning how to fly and were entering a new experience; that of learning how to make use of our flying ability in actual service. We would no longer be floating around the airdrome in machines whose only purpose was to stand up under the hard knocks of inexperienced pilots; but we were going to fly planes which had an actual military value in warfare.

===Chinese===
1924年9月，我们转场到了凯利机场。盼望了半年的日子终于到来。我们已度过单纯学飞行的阶段，正踏入一段新的经历：学习如何在实际勤务中运用自己的飞行本领。我们再也不用驾驶那些只为经得住生手粗猛操作而造的飞机在机场上空飘来荡去，而是要飞上真正具有实战军事价值的飞机了。

===Original===
We were old cadets and felt the importance of our experience. We were no longer treated as rookies but as potential officers. Before leaving Brooks we had conformed with cadet traditions and allowed groups of the new class to gather around us while we gravely spoke of examinations, check pilots, "Benzine Boards," and "washouts." We thoroughly enjoyed the awe inspired by our seventy-five hours of flying experience.

===Chinese===
我们是老学员了，深感自身资历的分量。人们不再把我们当新兵蛋子看待，而是当作预备军官。离开布鲁克斯之前，我们遵照学员传统，任由新一期的学员围拢过来，听我们正襟危坐地大谈考试、考核飞行员、"汽油委员会"（Benzine Board）和"淘汰生"。七十五个小时的飞行资历所唤起的敬畏，让我们受用至深。

===Original===
At Kelly our difficulties set in with renewed vigor. The De Havilands did not maneuver like the training Jennies, and we were required to fly as we had never flown before. If a cadet was not able to handle his ship in a maneuver which was at least equal to the standard, he was usually heading towards home within a week.

===Chinese===
到了凯利，困难又气势汹汹地找上门来。德·哈维兰操纵起来不像"珍妮"教练机那样听话，要求我们拿出前所未有的飞法。哪个学员操纵飞机的动作达不到起码的标准，通常不出一星期就得卷铺盖回家。

===Original===
We were allowed a few days to become accustomed to flying the new type of plane, then an instructor would go up with us to see if our progress had been satisfactory. If so we were sent to the next stage; if not we went up with a check pilot.

===Chinese===
我们先获准用几天时间适应新机型的飞行，随后教官会带飞一次，看我们的进步是否令人满意。满意，就送入下一阶段；不满意，就由考核飞行员带飞。

===Original===
From landings we went to the "eight" stage, where were assigned two landmarks such as a tree and a haystack several hundred feet apart, and required to do figure-eights around them. Then came the spot landing stage, when we throttled our engine at about a thousand feet and were required to land in a large white circle without using our motor. On this stage we were graded on our takeoff, climb, approach, landing, roll, and distance from mark, and method of handling the ship. In fact at Kelly we were constantly under observation and our only method of relaxation while flying was when the sky was cloudy and we could get above the clouds.

===Chinese===
降落之后是"8字"阶段：指定两个地标，比如一棵树和一座干草垛，相隔几百英尺，要求绕着它们飞8字。接着是定点降落阶段：在约一千英尺高度收油门，不得使用发动机，降落到一个大白圈里。这一阶段要给起飞、爬升、进场、接地、滑跑、离标记点的距离以及操纵飞机的方法逐项打分。事实上，在凯利我们时时处于监视之下，飞行中唯一的放松时刻，就是天有云、能爬到云层之上的时候。

===Original===
On one occasion we were flying with a low ceiling and the visibility was not very good. In fact it was an ideal day to do the things we were not supposed to. I was hedgehopping along over the country when I saw another DH playing around on my right. I flew over, and after chasing each other around for a while we proceeded to do chandelles, vertical banks, wing-overs, and everything else we could think of; all within a few feet of the ground as the clouds themselves were only about three hundred feet high. At last I decided to go up close to the other plane for a little low formation flying, but as I approached I saw that there were two men in the ship and that I had been breaking every rule ever established about low flying with an instructor watching me from another ship. I left that locality with wide open motor and for several days expected to be called on the carpet before the commanding officer on a washout offense. That instructor must have been a good sport, however, because I never heard from him and never was able to find out who he was.

===Chinese===
有一回，云幕很低，能见度也不佳。说实话，那天正适合干点明令不许干的事。我正贴着乡野掠地飞行，忽见另一架德·哈维兰在我右侧撒欢。我凑了过去，互相追逐了一阵之后，我们开始做急跃升转弯（chandelle）、垂直盘旋、跃升倒转，外加想得出来的一切花样；全部是在离地不过几英尺的高度——因为云层本身也只有三百英尺左右高。最后我决定贴近那架飞机来一次低空编队，可接近时才发现机上有两个人，原来那是位教官，正在另一架飞机上看着我大犯所有低空飞行的戒律。我开着全油门逃离了那片地方，随后一连几天都等着因淘汰级违规被叫到指挥官跟前训话。不过那位教官想必是个大度的人，因为我再没听到任何消息，也始终没能打听出他是谁。

===Original===
On another occasion, near the end of my course, I came very near being washed out for something I knew nothing about. I had been practicing landings in an S.E.5 on one corner of Kelly Field. When my time had expired, I landed on the pursuit stage, taxied up to the line, and turned the ship over to the mechanics. That afternoon I was called from class and ordered to report to the operations officer; whereupon he informed me that my flying days were over and that as I knew why, there was no use in explaining further. I was then ordered to report back to my studies.

===Chinese===
还有一回，临近课程尾声，我险些因为一件自己一无所知的事被淘汰。当时我在凯利机场一角用一架 S.E.5 练习降落。时间一到，我降落在驱逐机阶段场地，滑到停机线，把飞机交给机械师。当天下午我就被从课堂上叫出来，奉命去见作战军官；他随即告知我，我的飞行生涯到此为止，又说既然我自己知道原因，就不必再多解释了。接着我便被命令回去继续上课。

===Original===
It came out of a clear sky. I knew of a number of offenses I had committed but none of them at that time. I had actually no idea of what the operations officer was talking about.

===Chinese===
这真是晴天霹雳。我自知犯过不少过失，可当时一件也没犯。我实在不明白作战军官说的是什么。

===Original===
When school was over I returned to the operations hut and requested an account of the alleged offense. It appeared that the propeller on my S.E.5 was cracked, and the spreader board broken on the landing gear. The crew chief had reported this together with a statement that there were corn stalks hanging on the landing gear, and as there was no corn growing on Kelly Field, that was a sure sign that I had landed away from the airdrome without reporting the fact. A washout offense. We drove to the pursuit stage and found conditions exactly as stated, except that the corn stalks turned out to be weeds, and it was decided that the damage had been caused by a stake left standing in the corner of Kelly Field where I had been landing, although I had not felt the ship strike anything. The cadet who flew the plane earlier in the morning was using the same part of the field and said that he felt it strike a bump on one of his takeoffs but did not believe any damage had been done. Who was flying the ship made little difference, however, because as long as he had not landed away from the airdrome without authority, the slight damage was of no consequence. I had come very close to the "Benzine Board" for an offense of which I knew nothing, but it was probably only the open-mindedness and sense of fair play of the operations officer that kept me from being washed out as a result.

===Chinese===
放学后我回到作战室，要求说明所指违规的来龙去脉。原来是我那架 S.E.5 的螺旋桨裂了，起落架上的撑板断了。机械长上报时还附了一句：起落架上挂着玉米秆，而凯利机场不长玉米，这便确凿证明我曾擅自降落在机场之外且未报告——够得上淘汰级的违规。我们驱车赶到驱逐机阶段场地，发现情况与报告分毫不差，只是"玉米秆"原来是杂草；最后认定损伤是我降落地段——凯利机场一角——残留的一根木桩所致，尽管我并没觉得飞机撞过什么。清晨飞过这架飞机的学员用的是同一片场地，他说有一次起飞感到磕了一下，但以为没造成损伤。不过飞机是谁飞的并不重要：只要他没有未经批准在机场外降落，这点轻微损伤就无关紧要。我就这样为一件自己一无所知的违规险些闹到"汽油委员会"跟前，而最终没被淘汰，多半亏了那位作战军官的明达与公道。

===Original===
One day during the beginning of our term at Kelly, someone decided that the cadets should stand reveille. How it came about or who caused the decision was never known by the detachment, but there was a strong rumor circulated to the effect that our beloved Cadet Sergeant had not forgotten the episode of the polecats. It was an unheard of thing for the cadets of Kelly to stand formations. We had graduated from that when we left Brooks, and the thought of continuing it in our advanced status was, we concluded, degenerating to the morale of the detachment.

===Chinese===
我们到凯利开学后不久的某一天，不知是谁决定学员应当出早操应起床号。这事如何发生、由谁促成，全队始终无人知晓，但坊间流传着一种言之凿凿的说法：我们敬爱的学员军士并没有忘记臭鼬事件。凯利的学员还要列队，这是闻所未闻的事。离开布鲁克斯时我们就已经"毕业"于列队了；大家断定，在进阶身份下继续出操，有损全队的士气。

===Original===
Consequently, when our first sergeant himself delighted us with verbal visions of being tumbled out of bed at first call if we were not up at the blast of his whistle, we decided that if it were in the combined power of the detachment, the first call should not sound the next morning. We could not disobey an order; army training banishes even the remotest thought of that; but we might prevent that order from being given. The Cadet Captain and first sergeant were assigned to a private room together. The rest of us were given cots in the barracks. While supper was in progress that night the hands on the sergeant's alarm clock were so manipulated that the alarm would sound exactly one hour after the time set. At two o'clock the next morning a padlock was placed on the hasp outside of his door, and when first call blew a few hours later the cadet detachment slept soundly on.

===Chinese===
于是，当上士亲自用绘声绘色的言语款待我们——说要是哨声一响还不起床，头遍号一吹就把我们从被窝里掀出来——我们便打定主意：只要全队同心，第二天早晨那声头遍号就休想吹响。抗命是不行的；军旅训练连这种念头都斩尽杀绝；但我们可以让那道命令下不成。学员队长和上士被安排同住一个单间，我们其余人睡营房行军床。当晚晚餐进行之际，上士闹钟的指针被人做了手脚，闹铃会恰好比设定时间晚一小时才响。次日凌晨两点，他门外的搭扣被扣上了一把挂锁；几小时后头遍号吹响时，学员队依旧睡得香甜。

===Original===
From spot landings we passed to hurdles. Hurdles require the ship to be brought down without assistance from the engine, and after just passing over a line stretched about eight feet above the ground, to be landed as close as possible to the hurdle. This gave us excellent practice for landing over a fence in a small field.

===Chinese===
定点降落之后是跳栏训练。跳栏要求飞机不借助发动机动力下滑，在恰好越过一条离地约八英尺的横线之后，尽可能近地落在跳栏旁。这为我们练习在小场地越过围栏降落提供了绝佳的训练。

===Original===
One of the traditions at Kelly was that anyone knocking down the hurdle must treat the rest to a case of refreshments. It often happened that a pilot was so intent on getting over the hurdle string that he did not notice that his plane was in a stall, and about the time he was over the hurdle the bottom would fall out from under him and his plane would pancake into the ground. Almost every class had one or two minor crack-ups as a result of stalling over the hurdle string.

===Chinese===
凯利的一条传统是：撞倒跳栏者必须请全队喝一箱饮料消暑。常见的情况是，飞行员一心盯着过栏线，没留意飞机已经失速，等他刚过跳栏，脚下便"塌了底"，飞机平拍在地面上。几乎每期都有一两起因过栏失速造成的小事故。

===Original===
The De Havilands were not considered safe for hard stunting and as a result we were only allowed to do wing-overs and split air turns. Diving in excess of one hundred and fifty miles per hour was also forbidden. Consequently only air work allowing us to be thoroughly accustomed to the plane was included in the flying schedule before our formation training began.

===Chinese===
德·哈维兰被认为经不起剧烈特技，因此我们只获准做跃升倒转和分解转弯（split air turn），时速超过一百五十英里的俯冲也在禁止之列。所以在编队训练开始之前，课表上只安排了让我们充分熟悉机性能的空中练习。

===Original===
The strange field landing training was one of the most interesting parts of our schooling. An instructor would lead a number of planes and land in some field we had never seen before. Then each cadet was required to land and take off after the instructor. Some of the fields were small and full of obstructions. Yet we had comparatively few even minor crack-ups. Later each cadet was given an opportunity to lead the rest and pick out a field for them to land in while the instructor trailed.

===Chinese===
陌生场地降落训练是学业中最有趣的部分之一。教官带领数架飞机，降落在一块我们从未见过的场地上，然后每个学员依次跟着降落、起飞。有些场地狭小且障碍重重，但我们连轻微事故都很少发生。后来每个学员都轮到一次带领队友、替他们挑选降落场地的机会，教官则殿后压阵。

===Original===
At Kelly we were given more and longer cross country trips than at Brooks. One of the most important parts of flying training is cross country experience. We made flights to Corpus Christi, Galveston, Laredo and a number of other places.

===Chinese===
在凯利，长途转场飞行比布鲁克斯更多也更远。飞行训练中最重要的科目之一就是转场经验。我们飞过科珀斯克里斯蒂、加尔维斯顿、拉雷多等许多地方。

===Original===
Each class spent about two weeks on a gunnery expedition at Ellington Field between Houston and Galveston. Ellington Field was one of the few double fields built during the war, but was later abandoned and, except for a National Guard squadron, was entirely deserted.

===Chinese===
每期学员都要在休斯敦与加尔维斯顿之间的埃灵顿机场（Ellington Field）进行约两周的射击训练远征。埃灵顿机场是大战期间修建的少数双机场之一，后来被废弃，除了一支国民警卫队中队外，全然荒置。

===Original===
We set up our mess in the clubhouse and made the old building which had served as officers' quarters as comfortable as possible. This was in winter and the weather was cold, even in Texas, unusual though it might have been. There were no stoves available so we contrived all sorts of makeshifts to hold a little fire in. If nothing better was obtainable, we shovelled several inches of earth on the floor and devised a hood of some kind leading through a few lengths of tin pipe to the chimney. Of course these fires could not be left unguarded, so it was necessary to put them out in the morning to be rekindled at the close of operations for the day.

===Chinese===
我们把伙房设在会所里，又把那座曾作军官营房的旧楼收拾得尽量舒适。时值冬季，天气寒冷，哪怕在得克萨斯，这样的冷天也算少见。没有炉子，我们便各式土法齐上，好生起一小炉火。实在找不到更好的家什，就在地板上铺几英寸厚的土，再就地取材做个罩子，接上几节白铁管通到烟囱。这种火自然不能无人看管，于是早晨必须熄掉，等当天飞行结束后再重新点燃。

===Original===
Our gunnery work was divided into three parts: ground targets, shadow targets and tow targets. The ground targets were large sheets of paper similar to those used on a rifle range and were set up at an angle on the ground. We shot at these with both the Browning and Lewis machine guns.

===Chinese===
我们的射击训练分三部分：地面靶、影子靶和拖靶。地面靶是类似步枪靶场所用的大张靶纸，斜支在地上。我们用勃朗宁（Browning）和刘易斯（Lewis）两种机枪对其射击。

===Original===
The Browning guns on a De Haviland were mounted rigidly in front of the pilot and were synchronized with the engine to shoot between the blades of the propeller. They were capable of firing up to twelve hundred rounds a minute, depending on the motor rpm when they were fired.

===Chinese===
德·哈维兰上的勃朗宁机枪固定安装在飞行员正前方，与发动机同步，子弹从螺旋桨桨叶之间射出。每分钟射速最高可达一千二百发，视射击时的发动机转速而定。

===Original===
Several of us would form a large circle with our planes, and starting our dive from about one thousand feet, would fire short bursts into the target on the ground. After completing our bursts we would zoom back up into the circle while the next ship started its dive. Each plane had its individual target.

===Chinese===
我们几架飞机围成一个大圈，从约一千英尺高度开始俯冲，向地面上的靶子打几个短点射。点射完毕便急升回到圈中，下一架随即开始俯冲。每架飞机各有自己的靶子。

===Original===
After emptying the Browning guns we gave our observers a chance with their Lewises by circling low around the targets. On the next flight the pilot and observer traded places.

===Chinese===
打空勃朗宁机枪的弹药后，我们便低空绕靶盘旋，让观察员们用他们的刘易斯机枪过过瘾。下一轮飞行时，飞行员与观察员互换位置。

===Original===
The Lewis gun is mounted on a turret on the rear cockpit. Two guns were usually used together and they could be pointed in any direction.

===Chinese===
刘易斯机枪安装在后座舱的转架上，通常双枪联装，可以指向任何方向。

===Original===
After a few days on ground targets we were sent out over Trinity Bay for shadow targets. One plane is flown fairly high over the water while another fires at its shadow. The splashes from the bullets are easily seen and the accuracy of marksmanship very apparent.

===Chinese===
打了几天地面靶后，我们被派到特里尼蒂湾（Trinity Bay）上空打影子靶。一架飞机在水面上空较高的位置飞行，另一架朝它在水面上的影子开火。子弹溅起的水花清晰可见，射击精度一目了然。

===Original===
The tow targets are by far the most difficult of the three varieties, and require skillful maneuvering and excellent marksmanship. They consist of a cloth sleeve similar to a wind sock which is towed a few hundred feet behind a De Haviland flying at sixty or sixty-five miles an hour.

===Chinese===
拖靶是三种靶中最难打的，需要娴熟的操纵和精准的枪法。它是一条类似风向袋的布筒，拖曳在以每小时六十或六十五英里飞行的德·哈维兰身后几百英尺处。

===Original===
When the forward or Browning guns were used, the attacking ship approached the tow target head on, firing one or two short bursts as it passed. In this way there was no danger of the occupants of the towing plane being struck by a wild shot. The De Havilands were much too large to use the forward guns effectively on a tow target. Any accurate shooting required the quick maneuverability of a pursuit ship.

===Chinese===
使用前射勃朗宁机枪时，攻击机迎面接近拖靶，在掠过时打一两个短点射。这样拖曳机上的人员便没有流弹误中之虞。但德·哈维兰机体太大，用前射机枪打拖靶效果有限，任何精准射击都非得靠驱逐机的灵巧机动不可。

===Original===
The Lewis guns were used while flying parallel with the target and were very effective. When we were close enough we could often see the tracers pass directly through the cloth sleeve.

===Chinese===
刘易斯机枪则在与目标平行飞行时使用，非常有效。距离够近时，我们常能看见曳光弹径直穿入布筒。

===Original===
After returning from Ellington Field we were given a few hours in each of the various types of service airplanes. The MB-3 and the S.E.5 scouts; the Martin Bombers with their twin Liberty engines; the TW-5 two-place transition planes; and the little Sperry messengers. In this way we obtained experience in each branch: pursuit, attack, observation and bombardment. Later we were given our choice of which we desired to specialize in. If our wishes corresponded with the judgment of the instructors we were assigned to that branch.

===Chinese===
从埃灵顿机场回来后，我们得以在各种现役机型上各飞几个小时：MB-3 与 S.E.5 侦察机，装双"自由"式发动机的马丁轰炸机，TW-5 双座过渡教练机，还有小巧的斯佩里"信使"式（Sperry messenger）。这样我们便获得了各个兵种——驱逐、攻击、观测与轰炸——的亲身体验。随后让我们自选专精方向，若所选与教官们的判断相符，便被分到那个兵种。

===Original===
Together with three other cadets and four student officers, I was sent to the pursuit stage, where we spent the few remaining weeks of our course, piloting the S.E.5 and the MB-3 single seaters.

===Chinese===
我与另外三名学员及四名见习军官一起被送往驱逐机阶段，在那里驾驶 S.E.5 和 MB-3 单座机度过了课程所剩的几个星期。

===Original===
Pursuit combines a little of every branch of the air corps. In addition to formation combat, dog fighting, and ground strafing, the pursuit pilot is often called upon to make observations and do light bombing.

===Chinese===
驱逐汇聚了航空兵各兵种的一点本事。除了编队空战、空中格斗和低空扫射之外，驱逐机飞行员还常要执行观测和轻型轰炸任务。

===Original===
A great deal of our time was devoted to formation flying. Air combat of the future will probably often be between large formations rather than individual pilots, and it is accordingly of utmost importance for the pursuit pilot to hold his place in formation instinctively, so that his entire attention can be devoted to the enemy rather than to his own formation.

===Chinese===
我们的大量时间用于编队飞行。未来的空战大概常在大型编队之间而非单个飞行员之间展开，因此驱逐机飞行员必须本能地守住自己在编队中的位置，以便把全部注意力投向敌机，而不是自己的编队，这一点至关重要。

===Original===
We often maneuvered our flights while the individual planes were less than ten feet apart and it was not unusual to dive vertically for several thousand feet in a fairly close formation.

===Chinese===
我们常常在单机间距不足十英尺的状态下做编队机动，以相当密集的队形垂直俯冲几千英尺也不稀奇。

===Original===
We learned the use of Lufberry circles, cross over turns, and other formation tactics. Our formations were tight, it is true, but strange as it may seem, very few accidents occur from too close flying. A pilot is constantly alert when his plane is only a short distance from the one in front and nothing is allowed to distract his attention. On the other hand, when there is quite some distance separating them he is often more engrossed in lighting a cigarette or watching some object on the ground than in his own formation.

===Chinese===
我们学习了卢贝里圆阵（Lufberry circle）、交叉转弯及其他编队战术。我们的队形确实很密，但说来奇怪，因飞得过近而出的事故极少。当前机近在咫尺时，飞行员时刻绷紧神经，不容任何东西分他的心；反之，间距一拉开，他往往更专注地点烟或盯着地上的什么东西，反倒把自己的编队忘在脑后。

===Original===
In pursuit flying we came to have great confidence in our parachutes. The planes we were flying were kept in excellent condition and none ever failed, notwithstanding the fact that we placed them under every conceivable strain imaginable. But the knowledge that we did not have to concern ourselves about whether they did fall apart or not was an invaluable factor in building up our morale. Our formations were tighter, the combats faster, and our flying better as a result.

===Chinese===
在驱逐机飞行中，我们对降落伞渐渐产生了极大的信任。我们驾驶的飞机保养得极好，尽管我们把它折腾到无以复加的地步，却从未有一架散架。而"不必担心它是否会散架"这份底气，是砥砺士气无可估量的因素。正因如此，我们的队形更密，空战更快，飞行也更出色。

===Original===
We had a number of close calls but considering the amount of flying we had done, and that all of it was military flying, which cannot be ever compared to commercial traffic as far as safety is concerned, our accidents were remarkably few and none resulted seriously.

===Chinese===
我们有过不少千钧一发的时刻，但考虑到我们的飞行总量，而且全部是军事飞行——就安全而论，那根本无法与商业运输相提并论——我们的事故少得出奇，且没有一起造成严重后果。

===Original===
No one knows of the risk he takes better than the pursuit pilot and no one is less concerned about it. Every move, although at lightning speed, is made with a coolness born of experience and love of flying. The army Air Corps is built up of men who fly for the love of flying. Their only mission in life is to build up the finest air corps in the world, and their greatest desire is to be given the opportunity to do so without restriction. If an officer is lost in duty he would be the last one to wish for resulting restrictions on his comrades.

===Chinese===
没有人比驱逐机飞行员更清楚自己所冒的风险，也没有人比他更不把它放在心上。每一个动作虽然快如闪电，却带着源于经验与飞行之爱的冷静。陆军航空兵团正是由一群为热爱而飞行的人组成的。他们此生唯一的使命，就是建成全世界最精锐的航空兵团；他们最大的心愿，就是能不受掣肘地放手去干。倘若一名军官殉职于职守，他最不愿意看到的，就是战友们因此受到掣肘。

===Original===
A week of our pursuit training was spent on a gunnery expedition at Galveston. We flew there from Kelly Field in MB-3A machines and fired on tow targets exclusively. Our field was close to the Gulf, and when the day's operations had been completed we were free to go about as we chose. Consequently a large part of the evening was spent along the rocky beach.

===Chinese===
驱逐机训练中有一周是在加尔维斯顿的射击远征中度过的。我们从凯利机场驾驶 MB-3A 飞到那里，专打拖靶。我们的驻地紧邻墨西哥湾，一天的训练结束后便可自由活动。于是，晚间的大部分时光都消磨在岩石海滩上。

===Original===
On the night of our last day at Galveston several of us were holding a contest to decide which could reach the most distant rock between the breakers, before the next wave rolled in. One of the fellows was outstanding in his accomplishments. In fact he was so dextrous that none of us could compete, so we were all loud in our praises and unanimously agreed that there was not a rock in the gulf too obscure for him. There was, however, a rock a number of feet beyond the most distant point any of us had attained, which was visible only for an instant as the last breaker receded and before the next arrived. Even this was possible, we confidently assured him.

===Chinese===
在加尔维斯顿的最后一夜，我们几个人举行了一场比赛，看谁能在下一排浪卷来之前，踩到浪花之间最远的那块岩石。其中一位伙伴的成绩出类拔萃。事实上他身手矫捷得让我们无人能敌，于是大家交口称赞，一致认定湾里没有一块石头能难得住他。不过，在我们任何人所及的最远点再往外数英尺处，确实还有一块岩石，只有当前一排浪退尽、后一排浪未至的那一瞬间才露出身影。我们信心十足地向他保证：就连这块，他也能拿下。

===Original===
He watched that rock intently for several minutes; then bolstered up by our praise and his own confidence, he stood poised and ready. At the proper moment he nimbly leaped from boulder to boulder after the retreating surf but just before the final rock was touched a large wave towered above it. Too late! The chance of retreat had never been considered and its opportunity had passed. With do or die determination he leaped onto the boulder and into the breaking wave. This incident would not have been serious or its consequences important had we been able to carry any extra equipment in our pursuit planes, but as it was, extra clothing was a scarce article, and when we took off for San Antonio and Kelly the following morning, it was necessary for him to send his wet clothes back in a De Haviland and make his flight in a bearskin flying suit without insulation against the bearskin.

===Chinese===
他目不转睛地盯了那块岩石好几分钟；随后在我们的赞誉和自信的双重加持下，他蓄势待发。时机一到，他便追着退浪在礁石间轻盈跳跃，可就在触到最后那块岩石之前，一排大浪已在那上方高高耸起。太迟了！撤退的选项从未进入考虑，机会也已溜走。他抱着破釜沉舟的决心纵身跃上礁石，扎进碎浪之中。倘若驱逐机还能多带些装备，这桩事本无足轻重，可当时换洗衣物是稀罕物，第二天早晨我们起飞返回圣安东尼奥和凯利时，他只好把湿衣服交由一架德·哈维兰捎回去，自己光着身子套一件熊皮飞行服飞行——皮子底下别无衬隔。

===Original===
In warm weather these suits acquired an odor similar to that of a goat which has been in the barn all winter and the fur itself was far from comfortable. On the trip back a piston froze in the engine. For two days the cadet was alternately roasting in the southern sun and freezing in the Texas nights while he guarded his ship and waited for a new engine.

===Chinese===
天气一暖，这种飞行服便会散发出一种类似在牲口棚里圈了一冬的山羊的气味，皮毛本身也远谈不上舒适。回程中，发动机里一个活塞卡死了。整整两天，那位学员守着飞机等新发动机，白天在南方烈日下烘烤，夜晚在得克萨斯寒夜里挨冻。

===Original===
After our return from Galveston while we were practicing formation attack on two seaters, I experienced one of the incidents of the military pilot's life. I made my first emergency parachute jump. When an Army plane crashes, the pilot is required to write a detailed report of the crash. My account was as follows:

===Chinese===
从加尔维斯顿回来后，在我们练习编队攻击双座机期间，我经历了军旅飞行员生涯中的一桩大事：我完成了第一次应急跳伞。陆军飞机坠毁后，飞行员须就事故提交一份详细报告。我的报告如下：

===Original===
"A nine-ship S.E.5 formation, commanded by Lieut. Blackburn, was attacking a DH-4B, flown by Lieut. Maughan at about a 5,000 foot altitude and several hundred feet above the clouds. I was flying on the left of the top unit, Lieut. McAllister on my right, and Cadet Love leading. When we nosed down on the DH, I attacked from the left and Lieut. McAllister from the right. After Cadet Love pulled up, I continued to dive on the DH for a short time before pulling up to the left. I saw no other ship nearby. I passed above the DH and a moment later felt a slight jolt followed by a crash. My head was thrown forward against the cowling and my plane seemed to turn around and hang nearly motionless for an instant. I closed the throttle and saw an S.E.5 with Lieut. McAllister in the cockpit, a few feet on my left. He was apparently unhurt and getting ready to jump.

===Chinese===
"由布莱克本中尉（Lieut. Blackburn）指挥的九机 S.E.5 编队，正在攻击一架由莫恩中尉（Lieut. Maughan）驾驶的 DH-4B，高度约5000英尺，在云层之上几百英尺。我在上层分队左侧，麦卡利斯特中尉（Lieut. McAllister）在我右侧，洛夫学员（Cadet Love）担任长机。我们向那架 DH 俯冲时，我从左翼攻击，麦卡利斯特中尉从右翼攻击。洛夫学员拉起后，我又对着 DH 继续俯冲了片刻，才向左侧拉起。我没有看见附近有别的飞机。我从那架 DH 上方掠过，片刻之后感到轻微一震，紧接着一声巨响。我的头向前撞在整流罩上，飞机仿佛打了个转，有一瞬几乎悬停不动。我关掉油门，看见一架 S.E.5 在我左侧几英尺外，座舱里是麦卡利斯特中尉。他显然没有受伤，正准备跳伞。

===Original===
"Our ships were locked together with the fuselages approximately parallel. My right wing was damaged and had folded back slightly, covering the forward right-hand corner of the cockpit. Then the ships started to mill around and the wires began whistling. The right wing commenced vibrating and striking my head at the bottom of each oscillation. I removed the rubber band safetying the belt, unbuckled it, climbed out past the trailing edge of the damaged wing, and with my feet on the cowling on the right side of the cockpit, which was then in a nearly vertical position, I jumped backwards as far from the ship as possible. I had no difficulty in locating the pull ring and experienced no sensation of falling. The wreckage was falling nearly straight down and for some time I fell in line with its path and only slightly to one side. Fearing the wreckage might fall on me, I did not pull the rip cord until I dropped several hundred feet and into the clouds. During this time I had turned one-half revolution and was falling flat and face downward. The parachute functioned perfectly; almost as soon as I pulled the rip cord the riser jerked on my shoulders, the leg straps tightened, my head went down, and the chute fully opened.

===Chinese===
"两架飞机绞锁在一起，机身大体平行。我的右翼已经损坏，微微向后折翻，盖住了座舱右前角。接着两机开始打着旋下坠，张线发出啸叫。右翼开始震颤，每摆到最低点就磕一下我的头。我扯掉固定安全带的橡皮圈，解开带扣，从受损机翼后缘爬出，双脚蹬在座舱右侧的整流罩上——此时它已近乎垂直——拼命向后跃离飞机。我毫不费力就摸到了开伞环，也没有坠落的感觉。残骸几乎笔直下坠，有一阵我正落在它的轨迹上，仅稍稍偏在一侧。我生怕残骸砸到自己，直到坠落了几百英尺、钻进云层才拉的开伞绳。这段时间里我转了半圈，呈水平姿态脸朝下坠落。降落伞工作得完美无缺；开伞绳几乎刚一拉，吊带就猛地拽住我的双肩，腿带绷紧，头沉了下去，伞完全张开了。

===Original===
"I saw Lieut. McAllister floating above me and the wrecked ships pass about 100 yards to one side, continuing to spin to the right and leaving a trail of lighter fragments along their path. I watched them until, still locked together, they crashed in the mesquite about 2,000 feet below and burst into flames several seconds after impact.

===Chinese===
"我看见麦卡利斯特中尉飘在我上方，而那两架缠在一起的残骸从侧旁约100码处坠过，仍不停向右旋转，沿途撒下一串轻质碎片。我一直目送着它们，直到它们依旧绞锁着砸进下方约2000英尺处的牧豆树丛，触地几秒后轰然起火。

===Original===
"Next I turned my attention to locating a landing place. I was over mesquite and drifting in the general direction of a plowed field which I reached by slipping the chute. Shortly before striking the ground, I was drifting backwards, but was able to swing around in the harness just as I landed on the side of a ditch less than 100 feet from the edge of the mesquite. Although the impact of landing was too great for me to remain standing, I was not injured in any way. The parachute was still held open by the wind and did not collapse until I pulled in one group of shroud fines.

===Chinese===
"接着我把注意力转到寻找落点上。我正处在牧豆树丛上空，正泛泛漂向一片犁过的田地，便操纵降落伞侧滑过去。快要触地时我在向后漂，但在落进一条水沟旁的一瞬间，我靠背带里的转身正对了过来，落点离牧豆树丛边缘不足100英尺。尽管接地冲力大得让我站不住，我却毫发无损。降落伞仍被风兜着，直到我收拢一组伞绳它才瘪塌下来。

===Original===
"During my descent I lost my goggles, a vest pocket camera which fitted tightly in my hip pocket, and the rip cord of the parachute."

===Chinese===
"下降途中，我丢掉了风镜、一台紧揣在后裤袋里的背心口袋相机，还有降落伞的开伞绳。"

===Original===
During the descent all the other planes broke formation and arched around us. Every ship within sight proceeded at full speed to the spot and before long the air was full of machines. Several of the De Havilands landed in the plowing and within half an hour two planes with extra parachutes were sent to take us back to Kelly. About an hour after the crash we had two new S.E.5's and were back in the air again.

===Chinese===
我下降时，其余飞机全部解散编队，绕着我们盘旋。视野之内的每一架飞机都全速赶赴出事地点，不多时空中便挤满了机器。几架德·哈维兰降落在犁地里，不出半小时，两架带着备用降落伞的飞机便被派来接我们回凯利。坠机后约一小时，我们已经领到两架崭新的 S.E.5，重新上了天。

===Original===
The parachute is a marvelous invention, experimented with as early as the 16th century by Leonardo da Vinci.

===Chinese===
降落伞是一项了不起的发明，早在16世纪，列奥纳多·达·芬奇（Leonardo da Vinci）就曾对它进行过试验探索。

===Original===
The first parachute was built by a Frenchman in 1784. This parachute was a rigid structure covered with very strong paper and fabric. It was used in a jump from a building in Paris.

===Chinese===
第一具降落伞由一位法国人于1784年制成。那是一具覆着高强度纸和织物的刚性结构，被用于从巴黎一座建筑物上跳下。

===Original===
About a year later the same type of parachute was dropped from a hot-air balloon in England. Soon jumps began to be made from balloons with other types of rigid parachutes.

===Chinese===
约一年之后，同一类型的降落伞在英格兰从一只热气球上投下。不久，人们便开始携带其他类型的刚性降落伞从气球上跳下。

===Original===
About 1880, Captain Thomas Baldwin made a name for himself by jumping from hot-air balloons with a chute which was a forerunner of the present type. He was the first really successful jumper, but success in those days was judged by how long a man lived in this profession.

===Chinese===
大约1880年，托马斯·鲍德温上尉（Captain Thomas Baldwin）凭借一种堪称现代降落伞雏形的伞具从热气球上跳伞而名声大噪。他是第一位真正成功的跳伞者，不过在那些年月，成功的标准是干这一行的人能活多久。

===Original===
In 1912, the first parachute jump from an airplane was made. The container was attached to the plane and the man who did the jumping pulled the parachute out as he fell.

===Chinese===
1912年，人们完成了第一次从飞机上跳伞。伞包装在飞机上，跳伞者在坠落过程中把降落伞拽出来。

===Original===
The war really proved that the parachute is a life saving apparatus for use with airplanes. Early in 1918 the allied pilots reported that German pilots were using parachutes to escape from their planes whenever they were out of control or set on fire. This was the beginning of insistent demands on the part of our allied pilots for parachute equipment. The A.E.F. tried to produce a satisfactory parachute by combining the good feature of several chutes already in existence. All of these, however, were very bulky and heavy and hard to get on the plane.

===Chinese===
大战真正证明了降落伞是配用于飞机的救生装备。1918年初，协约国飞行员报告说，德国飞行员每逢飞机失控或起火，便用降落伞逃生。协约国飞行员对降落伞装备的迫切要求由此发端。美国远征军（A.E.F.）试图融合当时几种伞具的优点，造出一种令人满意的降落伞，但那些伞全都笨重臃肿，很难装上飞机。

===Original===
During the summer of 1918, the U.S. Air Service officials appealed to Washington for good airplane parachutes. A large number of tests were made. Finally, after combining all the good points of foreign and American chutes, a satisfactory free type of parachute was developed. By free type I mean the kind of parachute which is entirely independent of the plane.

===Chinese===
1918年夏，美国航空队的官员们吁请华盛顿提供优良的航空降落伞。大量试验随之展开。最终，在融合了外国与美国伞具的全部优点之后，一种令人满意的自携式降落伞研制成功。所谓自携式，我指的是完全独立于飞机的那种降落伞。

===Original===
Stories often come out in the newspapers about parachutes that fail to open. What probably really happens is that men who make jumps from planes are killed before they are able to pull the rip cord which opens the parachute. In the past there was always a great deal of danger in testing out a new type of chute, but now they have been developed to such a high degree of efficiency that there are practically no fatalities. Each parachute that is used by the government is repacked every month and tested every six months.

===Chinese===
报纸上时常刊出降落伞打不开的报道。真实情况多半是：跳伞者在来得及拉动开伞绳之前就已经毙命。过去测试新型降落伞总是险象环生，如今伞具的可靠程度已臻完善，几乎不再有死亡事故。政府使用的每一具降落伞每月重新包装一次，每六个月检测一次。

===Original===
Altogether, about 57 lives have been saved by parachutes in government service. In every instance the jump took place because of fog, engine failure while flying over unfavorable country, collision of planes or other very definite emergencies. They say in the service that any flyer who jumps to save his life becomes a member of the "Caterpillar Club." This is because the parachute is made entirely of silk, and silk comes from caterpillars. All the 57 members of this club feel that their lives have been saved by the silkworm caterpillar!

===Chinese===
总计起来，政府服役中的降落伞已拯救了约57条生命。每一次跳伞都事出有因：或因大雾，或因在险恶地域上空发动机失灵，或因飞机相撞，或因其他十分明确的紧急状况。军中相传，凡为保命而跳伞的飞行员，都自动成为"毛虫俱乐部"（Caterpillar Club）的会员。这是因为降落伞全用丝绸制成，而丝出于蚕。这个俱乐部的全体57名成员都觉得，救他们一命的是蚕宝宝！

===Original===
There is a saying in the service about the parachute: "If you need it and haven't got it, you'll never need it again!" That just about sums up its value to aviation.

===Chinese===
军中有一句关于降落伞的俗话："你需要它时若没有，往后再也用不着了！"它对航空的价值，尽在这句话里。

===Original===
For two of the last days we were on tactical maneuvers with the other branches. Half of our number were assigned to defend the bombers and observation planes while the other half attacked them. When we met, a lively combat ensued and the air would be full of pursuit planes in every conceivable position, each trying to get on the tail of an enemy plane without being first shot down itself.

===Chinese===
最后两天里，我们与其它兵种进行战术演习。半数人被派去护卫轰炸机和观测机，另一半则扮演攻击方。两队相遇，一场酣战随之而起，满天都是姿态百出的驱逐机，每一架都想咬住敌机尾部，又不想自己先被击落。

===Original===
At night in the barracks we would argue about which side won the war, but whenever one of us would demonstrate to the enemy that he had been shot down in battle, another would interpose the claim that he had put the attacking ship out of commission several minutes previous to the combat.

===Chinese===
夜里回到营房，我们会为哪一方打赢了这场"战争"争个不休；可每当有人向"敌方"证明他早在空战中被击落时，总会有另一位插话，声称早在交火前几分钟，他就已经把那架攻击机打得退出了战斗。

===Original===
When graduation day arrived eighteen of us remained of the hundred and four cadets who started the course at Brooks a year before. We were presented with our wings and commissioned second lieutenants in the Air Service Reserve Corps. That night we gave a farewell dinner in San Antonio and for the last time assembled together.

===Chinese===
毕业之日到来时，一年前在布鲁克斯开班的一百零四名学员只剩下我们十八人。我们被授予飞行员翼章，受任为航空队后备军团的少尉。当晚我们在圣安东尼奥举行了告别晚宴，最后一次聚首。

===Original===
The next day we departed from Kelly.

===Chinese===
次日，我们挥手作别凯利。

## IX San Diego–St. Louis–New York / 九 圣迭戈—圣路易斯—纽约

===Original===
The transatlantic nonstop flight between New York and Paris was first brought into public consideration by Raymond Orteig who, in 1919, issued a challenge to the Aeronautical world by offering a prize of $25,000 to the first successful entrant. Details of the flight were placed in the hands of the National Aeronautic Association and a committee was appointed to form and administer the rules of the undertaking.

===Chinese===
纽约与巴黎之间的跨大西洋不着陆飞行，最早是由雷蒙德·奥泰格（Raymond Orteig）引入公众视野的。1919 年，他向航空界发出挑战，悬赏两万五千美元，奖给首位成功完成此航程的挑战者。飞行的细则交由全国航空协会（National Aeronautic Association）掌管，并指定了一个委员会来制定和管理这项壮举的规则。

===Original===
I first considered the possibility of the New York–Paris flight while flying the mail one night in the fall of 1926. Several facts soon became outstanding. The foremost was that with the modern radial air-cooled motor, high lift airfoils, and lightened construction, it would not only be possible to reach Paris but, under normal conditions, to land with a large reserve of fuel and have a high factor of safety throughout the entire trip as well.

===Chinese===
我第一次考虑纽约—巴黎飞行的可能性，是在 1926 年秋的一个夜里送航邮的时候。几个事实很快凸显出来。首要的一点是：凭借现代气冷星型发动机、高升力翼型和轻量化的结构，不但有可能飞抵巴黎，而且在正常条件下还能带着大量剩余燃料降落，全程都有很高的安全系数。

===Original===
I found that there were a number of public spirited men in St. Louis sufficiently interested in aviation to finance such a project, and in December 1926 I made a trip to New York to obtain information concerning planes, motors, and other details connected with the undertaking.

===Chinese===
我发现圣路易斯有不少热心公益的人士，对航空的关注足以出资赞助这样一项计划。1926 年 12 月，我专程去了一趟纽约，搜集有关飞机、发动机及这项事业其他细节的信息。

===Original===
In connection with any important flight there are a number of questions which must be decided at the start, among the most important of which are the type of plane and the number of motors to be used. A monoplane, although just coming into general use in the United States, is much more efficient than a biplane for certain purposes due to the lack of interference between wings, and consequently can carry a greater load per square foot of surface at a higher speed. A single motored plane, while it is more liable to forced landings than one with three motors, has much less head resistance and consequently a greater cruising range. Also there is three times the chance of motor failure with a tri-motored ship, for the failure of one motor during the first part of the flight, although it would not cause a forced landing, would at least necessitate dropping part of the fuel and returning for another start.

===Chinese===
任何一次重要飞行，开头都有许多问题必须定夺，其中最重要的莫过于飞机的型式和所用发动机的台数。单翼机虽然当时在美国才刚开始普及，但由于上下翼之间没有干扰，在某些用途上效率远高于双翼机，因而能在更高速度下让每平方英尺翼面承载更大的载荷。单发动机飞机虽然比三发动机飞机更容易被迫降落，但迎面阻力小得多，巡航航程也因此大得多。而且三发飞机发生发动机故障的概率是单发的三倍：飞行前段若有一台发动机停车，虽不至于迫降，至少也得抛掉一部分燃料，返回后重新出发。

===Original===
The reliability of the modern air-cooled radial engine is so great that the chances of an immediate forced landing due to motor failure with a single motor, would in my opinion, be more than counterbalanced by the longer cruising range and consequent ability to reach the objective in the face of unfavorable conditions.

===Chinese===
现代气冷星型发动机的可靠性极高；依我看，单发动机飞行因发动机故障而随即迫降的那点风险，与更长的巡航航程、以及由此在不利条件下依然抵达目标的能力相比，利远大于弊。

===Original===
After careful investigation I decided that a single motored monoplane was, for my purpose, the type most suited to a long distance flight, and after two more trips to the east coast and several conferences in St. Louis, an order was placed with the Ryan Airlines of San Diego, California, on February 28, 1927, for a plane equipped with a Wright Whirlwind J5-C 200 hp radial air-cooled motor and Pioneer navigating instruments including the Earth Inductor Compass.

===Chinese===
经过仔细调研，我认定就我的目的而言，单发动机单翼机是最适合远程飞行的机型。又两度赴东海岸、并在圣路易斯开了几次会之后，1927 年 2 月 28 日，我们向加利福尼亚州圣迭戈的瑞安航空公司（Ryan Airlines）下了订单，订购一架飞机，配装莱特"旋风"J-5C 200 马力气冷星型发动机（Wright Whirlwind J5-C）和包括地磁感应罗盘（Earth Inductor Compass）在内的先锋牌（Pioneer）导航仪表。

===Original===
I went to San Diego to place the order and remained in California during the entire construction of the plane.

===Chinese===
我亲赴圣迭戈洽谈订单，并在飞机建造的整个期间留驻加利福尼亚。

===Original===
The personnel of the Ryan Airlines at once caught the spirit of the undertaking, and during the two months of construction the organization labored as it never had before. Day and night, seven days a week, the structure grew from a few lengths of steel tubing to one of the most efficient planes that has ever taken the air. During this time it was not unusual for the men to work twenty-four hours without rest, and on one occasion Donald Hall, the Chief Engineer, was over his drafting table for thirty-six hours.

===Chinese===
瑞安公司上下的员工立刻领会了这项壮举的精神，建造的两个月里，整个厂子的干劲前所未有。日夜兼程、每周七天，机体从几根钢管长成了有史以来最高效的飞机之一。这期间工人们一连二十四小时不休息并不稀奇，总工程师唐纳德·霍尔（Donald Hall）有一次伏在制图台前长达三十六个小时。

===Original===
I spent the greater part of the construction period working out the details of navigation and plotting the course, with its headings and variations, on the maps and charts. After working out the track on the gnomonic and Mercators charts, I checked over the entire distance from New York to Paris with the nautical tables. The flight from San Diego to St. Louis and from St. Louis to New York was comparatively simple, and I took the courses directly from the state maps.

===Chinese===
建造期的大部分时间，我都在推敲导航细节，把航线连同各段航向与磁差标绘在地图和海图上。先在心射投影图和墨卡托投影图上解出航迹，再用航海表核算了纽约到巴黎的全程。圣迭戈至圣路易斯、圣路易斯至纽约这两段相对简单，航向直接取自各州地图。

===Original===
From New York to Paris I worked out a great circle, changing course every hundred miles or approximately every hour. I had decided to replace the weight of a navigator with extra fuel, and this gave me about three hundred miles additional range. Although the total distance was 3,610 miles, the water gap between Newfoundland and Ireland was only about 1,850 miles, and under normal conditions I could have arrived on the coast of Europe over three hundred miles off of my course and still have had enough fuel remaining to reach Paris; or I might have struck the coastline as far north as Northern Scandinavia, or as far south as Southern Spain and landed without danger to myself or the plane, even though I had not reached my destination. With these facts in view, I believed the additional reserve of fuel to be more important on this flight than the accuracy of celestial navigation.

===Chinese===
纽约至巴黎一段，我按大圆航线求解，每隔一百英里、也就是大约每小时改变一次航向。我已决定用额外的燃料顶替领航员的重量，这使航程增加了约三百英里。全程虽达 3,610 英里，纽芬兰与爱尔兰之间的水域却只有约 1,850 英里；正常情况下，即便抵达欧洲海岸时偏离航线三百多英里，我仍能有足够剩余燃料飞到巴黎；或者我可能在远至北斯堪的纳维亚、南至西班牙南部的地方碰上海岸线，即使没到目的地，也能安全降落，人机俱无危险。鉴于这些，我认为在这次飞行中，多带备份燃料比天文导航的精度更为重要。

===Original===
For the flight from San Diego to St. Louis and New York I carried maps of the individual states and one of the United States with the course plotted on each. For the flight from New York to Paris I had two hydrographic charts of the North Atlantic Ocean containing the great circle course and its bearing at intervals of one hundred miles. In addition to these charts, I had a map of each state, territory and country passed over. This included maps of Connecticut, Rhode Island, Massachusetts, Nova Scotia, Newfoundland, Ireland, England and France. Also a map of Europe.

===Chinese===
圣迭戈至圣路易斯至纽约一段，我携带了各州地图和一张标好航线的美国全图。纽约至巴黎一段，我有两张北大西洋海图，上面绘有大圆航线及其每隔一百英里的方位。除海图外，我还有飞经的每个州、领地和国家的地图，包括康涅狄格、罗得岛、马萨诸塞、新斯科舍、纽芬兰、爱尔兰、英格兰和法国的地图，另有一张欧洲地图。

===Original===
I expected to be able to locate my position approximately on the coast of Europe by the terrain. Ireland is somewhat mountainous; England rather hilly on the southern end; France is a lowland along the coast; Spain is mountainous. Therefore the coastline should indicate the country, and my accurate position could be obtained by the contours of that coastline and by the position of towns, rivers and railroads.

===Chinese===
我预计凭地形就能在欧洲海岸上大致判定自己的位置。爱尔兰略有山峦；英格兰南端相当多丘陵；法国沿海是低地；西班牙多山。因此海岸线的形态应能指示所在国家，再由海岸线轮廓以及城镇、河流和铁路的位置定出准确位置。

===Original===
During the time of construction it was necessary to arrange for all equipment to be carried on the flight; including equipment for emergency use in a forced landing. After the first few hours there would be enough air in the fuel tanks to keep the ship afloat for some time. I also carried an air raft which could be inflated in several minutes and which could weather a fairly rough sea.

===Chinese===
建造期间还得备齐飞行携带的全部装备，包括迫降时应急使用的装备。头几个小时过后，油箱里存留的空气足以让飞机漂浮一段时间。我还带了一只充气救生筏，几分钟即可充好气，能扛住相当汹涌的海面。

===Original===
In addition to food for the actual flight, I carried five tins of concentrated Army rations each of which contained one day's food and which could be made to last much longer if necessary. I carried two canteens of water; one containing a quart for use during the actual flight and the other containing a gallon for emergency. In addition to this water, I had an Armburst cup which is a device for condensing the moisture from human breath into drinking water. The cup is cloth covered and contains a series of baffle plates through which the breath is blown. The cup is immersed in water and then removed and blown through. The evaporation of the water on the outside cools the cup walls and baffle plates on which the breath moisture collects and runs down to the bottom of the cup.

===Chinese===
除实际飞行途中的食物外，我还带了五听浓缩军用口粮，每听是一天的食量，必要时可以撑得更久。我带了两壶水：一壶一夸脱，供飞行途中使用；一壶一加仑，以备应急。除这些水之外，我还有一只阿姆伯斯特杯（Armburst cup），那是一种把人呼出的潮气冷凝成饮用水的装置。杯外包着布，内有一连串挡板，呼气从中吹过。先把杯子浸入水中，取出后再对它吹气。杯外水分的蒸发使杯壁和挡板冷却，呼气中的潮气便在其上凝结，流到杯底。

===Original===
The following is a list of the equipment carried on the flight:
- 2 Flashlights
- 1 Ball of string
- 1 Ball of cord
- 1 Hunting Knife
- 4 Red flares sealed in rubber tubes
- 1 Match safe with matches
- 1 larger needle
- 1 Canteen—4 qts.
- 1 ” —1 qt.
- 1 Armburst Cup
- 1 Air Raft with pump and repair kit
- 5 Cans of Army emergency rations
- 2 Air cushions
- 1 Hack saw blade

===Chinese===
以下是此次飞行携带的装备清单：
- 2 支手电筒
- 1 团细绳
- 1 团粗绳
- 1 把猎刀
- 4 支封在胶管里的红色焰弹
- 1 只装着火柴的防水火柴盒
- 1 枚大号缝针
- 1 只水壶——4 夸脱
- 1 只（同上）——1 夸脱
- 1 只阿姆伯斯特杯
- 1 只充气救生筏，附打气筒和修补包
- 5 听军用应急口粮
- 2 只气垫
- 1 条钢锯条

===Original===
Near the end of April the factory work was completed and early one morning, the 46 ft. wing was taken out of the second floor of the factory onto the top of a freight car and then lowered to a waiting truck by means of a gasoline crane. A few days later the plane was completely assembled in its hangar, and on April 28th, or sixty days after the order had been placed, I gave the *Spirit of St. Louis* her test flight. The actual performance was above the theoretical. The plane was off the ground in six and one-eighth seconds, or in 165 feet, and was carrying over 400 lbs. in extra gas tanks and equipment. The high speed was 130 mph and the climb excellent.

===Chinese===
四月底，工厂的活儿完工了。一天清晨，46 英尺长的机翼从工厂二楼吊出，放到一节棚车顶上，再用一台汽油起重机放落到等候的卡车上。几天后，飞机在机库里完成总装。4 月 28 日，即下单后的第六十天，我驾驶"圣路易斯精神号"（Spirit of St. Louis）进行了试飞。实际表现超出了理论值。飞机在六又八分之一秒内、也就是 165 英尺内离地，此时还载着 400 多磅的副油箱和装备。最高速度为每小时 130 英里，爬升性能极佳。

===Original===
The load tests were made from the old Camp Kearney parade-grounds near San Diego. At daybreak, one foggy morning, I took off from the field at Dutch Flats and headed for the Army's three kilometer speed course along Coronado Strand. The visibility became extremely bad over San Diego harbor and I was forced to land at Rockwell Field, North Island, and wait for the fog to lift before running the speed tests. The sun soon dispelled the fog and I took the plane four times over the speed course at an average of 128 mph in a slight cross wind. I was carrying about 25 gals. of gasoline and over 400 lbs. of extra tanks and equipment. On the way to Camp Kearney I ran a number of tests on the relation of motor rpm to air speed, and by the time I reached the old parade-grounds' field I had collected quite a bit of valuable test data.

===Chinese===
载荷测试在圣迭戈附近旧卡尼营（Camp Kearney）的阅兵场上进行。一个有雾的清晨，天刚破晓，我从达奇弗拉茨（Dutch Flats）的场地起飞，朝科罗纳多滩（Coronado Strand）沿线陆军的三公里测速航线飞去。圣迭戈港上空能见度变得极差，我被迫降落在北岛（North Island）的罗克韦尔机场（Rockwell Field），等雾散了再做测速。太阳很快驱散了雾，我驾机四次通过测速航线，在轻微侧风中平均时速 128 英里。机上载有约 25 加仑汽油和 400 多磅副油箱与装备。前往卡尼营途中，我做了一系列发动机转速与空速关系的测试，到达旧阅兵场场地时，已收集到不少宝贵的测试数据。

===Original===
I decided to run one more test before landing and had it about halfway completed when I allowed the data board to come too close to the window where a gust of air carried it out of the cockpit. I was flying over mesquite, over five miles from Camp Kearney, at about a 1,200-foot altitude at the time, and could only spiral around and watch the board flutter down into the top of a mesquite bush. There was a small clearing about 200 yards from the bush, in which it was possible to land a slow ship. I landed at Camp Kearney and sent for one of the cabin Hisso Standards used by the Ryan Airlines for their passenger service between San Diego and Los Angeles. When the Standard arrived I flew over and landed in the clearing near the lost board which was clearly visible from the air; but, after a fifteen minute search, I was unable to locate it from the ground in the thick mesquite. So I took off my coat and spread it over the top of another bush, then took the air again with the Standard to locate the board in relation to the coat.

===Chinese===
我决定落地前再做一个测试，做到一半时，我让数据板离舷窗太近，一阵风把它卷出了座舱。当时我正飞在牧豆树丛上空，离卡尼营五英里多，高度约 1,200 英尺，只能盘旋着，看那张板子飘飘荡荡落进一丛牧豆树的树冠。离那丛树约 200 码处有一小片空地，可以让一架低速飞机降落。我在卡尼营落地，派人去取瑞安公司用于圣迭戈—洛杉矶客运业务的一架舱式"希索"标准机（cabin Hisso Standard）。标准机一到，我就飞过去降落在遗失的数据板附近的空地上——从空中看它清清楚楚；可在茂密的牧豆树丛里，我找了十五分钟也没能从地面找到它。于是我脱下外套，铺在另一丛树的顶上，再驾着标准机升空，以外套为参照来确定数据板的位置。

===Original===
I had no difficulty in locating them both and found them to be about fifty yards apart. I landed again but could not locate the board, so moved my coat to the spot where I thought it should be and took off again. This time I had placed the coat within twenty feet of the data board, but it required several minutes' search in the thick mesquite to finally locate it.

===Chinese===
从空中找这两样东西毫不费力，发现它们相距约五十码。我再次落地，还是找不到数据板，便把外套挪到我以为它该在的位置，重新起飞。这一次外套离数据板已不到二十英尺，但在茂密的牧豆树丛中仍搜索了好几分钟，才终于找到它。

===Original===
After I returned to Camp Kearney with the Standard, we made preparations for the weight tests of the *Spirit of St. Louis*.

===Chinese===
我驾标准机回到卡尼营后，我们着手准备"圣路易斯精神号"的重量测试。

===Original===
The tests were to be made starting with a light load and increasing the weight carried by about fifty gallons of fuel for each test up to three hundred gallons, which was to be the maximum load tested. The plane passed its tests easily and took off with three hundred gallons in twenty seconds or 1,026 feet, and made a maximum speed of 124 miles per hour. The tests were made in a quartering wind varying from two to nine miles per hour and at an elevation of about six hundred feet above sea level.

===Chinese===
测试从轻载荷开始，每测一次增加约五十加仑燃料，直到三百加仑——这是测试的最大载荷。飞机轻松过关：载三百加仑起飞用时二十秒、滑跑 1,026 英尺，最大速度每小时 124 英里。测试在偏侧风中进行，风速每小时二至九英里，场地海拔约六百英尺。

===Original===
The final flight ended at dusk and the plane was left under guard on the field over night. The next morning, after most of the gasoline had been drained, I flew it back to Dutch Flats where final preparations were made for the flight to St. Louis.

===Chinese===
最后一轮飞行在黄昏结束，飞机留驻场上，派人看守过夜。第二天早晨，放掉大部分汽油后，我把它飞回达奇弗拉茨，在那里为飞往圣路易斯作最后的准备。

===Original===
I was delayed four days at San Diego by a general storm area over the United States that would greatly jeopardize the success of an overnight nonstop flight to St. Louis. From this flight I expected to obtain some very important data for use on the final hop from New York.

===Chinese===
我在圣迭戈耽搁了四天，因为一场大范围风暴正笼罩美国，会大大危及连夜直飞圣路易斯的成功。我指望从这次飞行中获取一些重要数据，供最后从纽约出发的航程使用。

===Original===
On the afternoon of May 9th, Dean Blake, Chief of the San Diego Weather Bureau, predicted favorable flying conditions for the succeeding day. The next morning I took the plane to Rockwell Field and at 3:55 p.m. Pacific time, I took off from North Island with 250 gallons of gasoline for the flight to St. Louis, escorted by two Army observation planes and one of the Ryan monoplanes. We circled North Island and San Diego, then headed on a compass course for St. Louis.

===Chinese===
5 月 9 日下午，圣迭戈气象局局长迪安·布莱克（Dean Blake）预报次日飞行条件良好。第二天上午，我把飞机转到罗克韦尔机场，太平洋时间下午 3 点 55 分，载着 250 加仑汽油从北岛起飞，前往圣路易斯，由两架陆军观测机和一架瑞安单翼机护航。我们绕北岛和圣迭戈盘旋一圈，随后按罗盘航向朝圣路易斯飞去。

===Original===
The ship passed over the first ridge of mountains, about 4,000 feet, very easily with reduced throttle. The escorting planes turned back at the mountains and I passed on over the desert and the Salton Sea alone. And at sunset I was over the deserts and mountains of Western Arizona.

===Chinese===
飞机收小油门，轻而易举地越过了约 4,000 英尺高的第一道山脊。护航机到山前折返，我独自继续飞越沙漠和索尔顿湖（Salton Sea）。日落时分，我已在亚利桑那西部的沙漠与群山之上。

===Original===
The moon was well above the horizon and with the exception of a short period before dawn I was able to distinguish the contour of the country the entire night. I flew a compass course, passing alternately over snow-capped ridges, deserts, and fertile valleys. One of the mountain ranges was over 12,000 feet high and completely snow covered. I cleared this range by about 500 feet and went on over the plains beyond.

===Chinese===
月亮早已升过地平线，除黎明前一小段时间外，我整夜都能辨认大地的轮廓。我按罗盘航向飞行，相继掠过积雪的山脊、沙漠和肥沃的河谷。其中一道山脉高逾 12,000 英尺，完全为雪所覆盖。我以约 500 英尺的余隙越过这道山脉，继续飞向前方的平原。

===Original===
The mountains passed quickly and long before daybreak I was flying over the prairies of Western Kansas. At dawn I located my position about twenty miles south of the course, just east of Wichita, Kansas. At 8:00 a.m. Central Standard time, I passed over Lambert Field and landed at 8:20 a.m., May 11th, fourteen hours and twenty-five minutes after leaving the Pacific Coast.

===Chinese===
群山很快抛在身后，离天亮还早，我已飞在堪萨斯西部的大草原上。拂晓时我判定自己的位置在航线以南约二十英里处、堪萨斯州威奇托（Wichita）正东。中部标准时间上午 8 点整，我飞越兰伯特机场，于 5 月 11 日上午 8 点 20 分降落——离开太平洋海岸已十四小时二十五分钟。

===Original===
The weather during the entire distance had been exactly as Dean Blake had predicted.

===Chinese===
全程的天气，与迪安·布莱克的预报分毫不差。

===Original===
At 8:13 the next morning (May 12th) I took off from Lambert Field for New York. The wind was west and the weather clear for the greater part of the distance. Over the Alleghenies, however, the sky was overcast and some of the mountain tops were in low hanging clouds and I followed the passes.

===Chinese===
次日上午 8 点 13 分（5 月 12 日），我从兰伯特机场起飞前往纽约。风向偏西，大半程天气晴朗。但在阿勒格尼山脉（Alleghenies）上空，天空阴云密布，一些山顶没入低垂的云中，我便沿山间隘路飞行。

===Original===
At 5:33 p.m. New York Daylight Saving time, I landed at Curtiss Field, Long Island.

===Chinese===
纽约夏令时间下午 5 点 33 分，我降落在长岛的柯蒂斯机场（Curtiss Field）。

## VI St. Louis / 六 圣路易斯

===Original===
At 8:17 a.m., Friday June 17th, Lindbergh hopped off in his plane for St. Louis. At Paterson he passed over the plant of the Wright Aeronautical Corporation where had been built the motor that had taken him across the Atlantic. At 11:16 he reached Columbus, Ohio. At Dayton he was joined by an escort of thirty fast Army planes. They took off from the field where the old hangar of Orville and Wilbur Wright still stands.

===Chinese===
6 月 17 日，星期五，上午 8 点 17 分，林德伯格驾驶自己的飞机起飞，前往圣路易斯。飞经帕特森时，他从莱特航空公司（Wright Aeronautical Corporation）的厂房上空掠过——载他飞越大西洋的那台发动机正是那里制造的。11 点 16 分，他抵达俄亥俄州哥伦布（Columbus, Ohio）。在代顿（Dayton），三十架快速的陆军飞机加入护航。它们起飞的那片场地，正是奥维尔·莱特与威尔伯·莱特（Orville and Wilbur Wright）的旧机库至今仍矗立之处。

===Original===
About 5 p.m. he approached St. Louis in a wet fog. He dropped lower and circled the city. As at New York the sky was dotted with planes. Streets and house tops were massed with people. As he landed at Lambert Field a cordon of troops protected him from the eager crowds.

===Chinese===
下午五点左右，他迎着湿雾飞近圣路易斯。他降低高度，绕城盘旋。同纽约一样，天空布满了飞机，街道和屋顶挤满了人群。他在兰伯特机场降落时，一道士兵的人链把他与热切的人群隔开。

===Original===
For the evening he managed to escape to the home of a friend where he got a little much-needed rest, though reporters and business solicitors still swarmed about him. Saturday morning came the huge city parade with luncheon and banquet to follow. Sunday he gave an exhibition flight over the old World's Fair grounds. Not an hour, scarcely a waking minute, was he free from demands upon his time and attention.

===Chinese===
当天晚上，他总算避进一位朋友家中，得到了一点亟需的休息，尽管记者和揽生意的人仍围着他团团转。星期六上午是盛大的全城游行，随后是午宴和晚宴。星期天，他在旧世博会会址上空作了一场表演飞行。没有一个钟头、几乎没有一个醒着的片刻，他不被各种事务索取着时间与心力。

===Original===
By this time his mail had exceeded the wildest imagination. It was estimated that more than 2,000,000 letters and several hundred thousand telegrams were sent him. He gave out the following statement:

===Chinese===
到这时，他的邮件已超出最狂野的想象。据估计，寄给他的信件超过 2,000,000 封，电报数十万份。他发表了如下声明：

===Original===
"To the Press: As an air mail pilot I deeply appreciate the sentiment which actuated my countrymen to welcome me home by air mail, and regret only that I have no way in which to acknowledge individually every one of the tens of thousands of air mail greetings I have received, for my heart is in the air mail service, and I would like to help keep alive the air-consciousness of America which my good fortune may have helped to awaken."

===Chinese===
"致新闻界：作为一名航邮飞行员，我深深感激同胞们以航空邮件欢迎我归国的这份情意。我唯一的遗憾是，收到的数以万计的航邮贺辞，我无法一一单独致谢。我的心在航邮事业之中，我的好运或许曾帮助唤醒美国的航空意识，我愿为让这种意识保持鲜活尽一份力。"

===Original===
By this time statisticians began to get busy. One official association estimated that the tremendous increase of interest in flying developed by Lindbergh's feat caused publications in the United States to use 25,000 tons of newsprint in addition to their usual consumption.

===Chinese===
到这时，统计学家们开始忙碌起来。据一家官方协会估计，林德伯格的壮举激发起飞行热望的巨大增长，使美国各类出版物在平常耗用之外，多消耗了 25,000 吨新闻纸。

===Original===
Roughly 5,000 poems were believed to have been written to commemorate the first New York to Paris flight. A town was named "Lindbergh." Scores of babies were reported christened after the flier. An enormous impetus was given the use of air mail.

===Chinese===
据信约有 5,000 首诗歌为纪念这第一次纽约—巴黎飞行而作。一座小镇被命名为"林德伯格"。据报道，几十名婴儿以这位飞行家的名字受洗。航空邮件的使用获得了巨大的推动。

===Original===
Inspired editorials were written in every part of the civilized world. The following from the *New York Times* suitably completes this very superficial record of the early Lindbergh welcome by mankind:

===Chinese===
文明世界的每个角落都写下了慷慨激昂的社论。下面这篇《纽约时报》（New York Times）的社论，恰可为人类欢迎林德伯格的这份浮光掠影的早期记录作结：

===Original===
"Such a man is one in a host. In treating of the psychology of those who adore Lindbergh it must first be set down that he has the qualities of heart and head that all of us would like to possess. When he left Newfoundland behind, the dauntless fellow seemed to have a rendezvous with Death, but his point of view was that he had an engagement in Paris. Two gallant Frenchmen had lost their lives, it was believed, in an attempt to fly across the Atlantic to the United States. An American, unknown to fame, in whom no one but himself believed, made the passage smoothly, swiftly and surely, traveling alone and almost unheralded. From New York to Paris, without a hand to clasp or a face to look into, was a deed to lose one's head over. And that's what everybody in France, Belgium and England proceeded to do.

===Chinese===
"这样的人万中无一。论及崇拜林德伯格者的心理，首先必须承认：他具备我们人人都渴望拥有的心智与品格。当他把纽芬兰抛在身后时，这个无畏的小伙子看似正与死神有约，可在他自己看来，那不过是巴黎有一场约会。两位英勇的法国人，据说已在飞越大西洋前往美国的尝试中丧生。而一个默默无闻、除自己之外无人看好的美国人，孤身一人、几乎无人预告地，平稳、迅捷而确切地渡过了大洋。从纽约到巴黎，一路没有一只可握的手、没有一张可凝视的脸，这样一件壮举足以让人为之神魂颠倒。法兰西、比利时和英格兰的每一个人，随即也都如此。

===Original===
"After all, the greater was behind—the young fellow's keeping his own head when millions hailed him as hero, when all the women lost their hearts to him, and when decorations were pinned on his coat by admiring Governments. Lindbergh had the world at his feet, and he blushed like a girl! A more modest bearing, a more unaffected presence, a manlier, kindlier, simpler character no idol of the multitude ever displayed. Never was America prouder of a son."

===Chinese===
"说到底，更了不起的还在后头——当千百万人向他欢呼英雄、当所有的女子为他倾心、当仰慕他的各国政府把勋章别上他的衣襟时，这个年轻人竟守住了自己的方寸。全世界都拜倒在他脚下，而他竟像个姑娘似的脸红！更谦逊的举止、更质朴的风度、更刚毅、更仁厚、更单纯的品格，从未在任何一个大众偶像身上显露过。美国从未为一个儿子这般自豪。"

## V Training at Brooks Field / 五 布鲁克斯机场的训练

===Original===
I arrived at Brooks Field on March 15th, 1924, but was not enlisted as a Flying Cadet until March 19th. Ordinarily a cadet enlists at the nearest station to his home and is given free transportation to his post of service and back to the enlistment point after his discharge. By enlisting at Brooks I was entitled to no transportation allowance except possibly bus fare back from Kelly where I graduated a year later.

===Chinese===
我于1924年3月15日抵达布鲁克斯机场，但直到3月19日才正式入伍为飞行学员。按惯例，学员应在离家最近的兵站入伍，享受前往服役驻地以及退伍后返回入伍地点的免费运送。我既然是在布鲁克斯入伍的，便无权享受任何运送津贴，顶多是一年后从毕业地凯利返回的汽车票钱。

===Original===
There were one hundred and four of us in all, representing nearly every state in the Union. We filled the cadet barracks to overflowing. There were two cots to each window and some of us were even quartered in the recreation hall. We were a carefree lot, looking forward to a year of wonderful experiences before we were graduated as second lieutenants and given our wings. Nearly all of us were confident that we would be there to graduate a year later. We had already passed the rigid physical and mental entrance examinations which so many of the other applicants had failed. We had no doubt of our ability to fly although most of us had never flown before, and we had yet to get our first taste of the life of a flying cadet.

===Chinese===
我们总共一百零四人，几乎联邦每个州都有代表。我们把学员营房塞得满满当当：每扇窗下两张行军床，有些人甚至被安置进了娱乐厅。这是一群无忧无虑的人，盼着在一年之后毕业授少尉衔、领翼章之前，先度过一年精彩的时光。我们几乎人人都自信一年后还能站在毕业的队列里。我们已然通过了体检与笔试那道严苛的入学关——多少别的报考者都栽在了那里。尽管大多数人从未飞过，我们却毫不怀疑自己的飞行能力；而飞行学员生活的滋味，我们还有待初次品尝。

===Original===
By the time we had been in the barracks a few hours stories began circulating around which originated from conversations with the last class of cadets who were waiting to be transferred to the advanced flying school at Kelly.

===Chinese===
进营房不过几个小时，种种传闻便开始不胫而走，都出自与上一期学员的攀谈——他们正等着转往凯利的高级飞行学校。

===Original===
Rumors of "Benzine Boards" and "washouts," "academic work" and "eight-hour examinations," "one eightys" and "check pilots," "walls with ears" and "cadet etiquette"—these and a hundred other strange terms were condescendingly passed down to us by the old cadets of six months experience. Someone remarked that less than forty percent of us could expect to finish the primary training at Brooks and that probably half of those would be washed out at Kelly.

===Chinese===
"汽油委员会"和"淘汰生"、"学业"和"八小时考试"、"一百八"和"考核飞行员"、"有耳朵的墙"和"学员礼法"——这些以及上百个陌生的字眼，被那批只有六个月资历的老学员居高临下地传授给我们。有人说到，能读完布鲁克斯初级训练的不足四成，而这些人里大概还有一半会在凯利被淘汰。

===Original===
By bed check that night we had already begun to feel the apprehension which is a part of a flying cadet's life from his first day at Brooks until he has received his pilot's wings at Kelly.

===Chinese===
当晚查铺时，我们已经开始体味那种忧惧——它是飞行学员生活的一部分，从踏进布鲁克斯的第一天起，直到在凯利领到飞行员翼章方休。

===Original===
Our actual flying training was to begin on the first of April. Two weeks were required to become organized and learn the preliminary duties of a cadet. During these two weeks we were inoculated against typhoid and smallpox at the hospital, taught the rudiments of cadet etiquette, given fatigue duty, required to police the grounds surrounding our barracks, inspected daily, and instructed and given examinations in five subjects. In our spare time we were allowed to look around the post or take the bus to San Antonio, provided, however, that we were back in bed not later than ten o'clock on Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday and Friday nights. At all other times we could stay out as late as we desired.

===Chinese===
真正的飞行训练定于4月1日开始。此前需要两周时间完成编组并学会学员的初步勤务。在这两周里，我们到医院接种了伤寒和天花疫苗，学了学员礼法的入门，服了杂役，打扫营房四周场地，每天接受检查，还要上课并接受五个科目的考试。闲暇时我们可以逛逛驻地，或搭公共汽车去圣安东尼奥，只是星期日、星期一、星期二、星期三、星期四和星期五这六个晚上，务必在十点前回到床上就寝。其余时间，我们爱在外面待多晚都行。

===Original===
When we did have a few spare moments in the afternoon, they were usually spent in trying to "chizzle" a hop from one of the instructors on the line.

===Chinese===
下午真有片刻闲暇时，多半都用来磨着停机线上的哪位教官，"蹭"一次飞行。

===Original===
Early one morning we were allowed to take the training ships out and push them to the line for the old cadets to fly. But when one of the planes nosed over after eight husky rookies heaved up on the hundred and fifty pound tail, it was decided to put us to work moving hydrogen cylinders for a balloon ascension.

===Chinese===
一天清晨，我们获准把教练机拖出机库，推到停机线上供老学员飞行。可八个壮硕的新兵蛋子一齐掀那一百五十磅重的机尾，居然把一架飞机掀得立了起来，于是上头决定改派我们去搬气球升空用的氢气瓶。

===Original===
As the first of April approached we were looking forward to the start of actual flying with great anticipation. Coupled with this was the anxiety of waiting for the returns from our examination papers, the failure of any two of which would be sufficient cause for their owner to be washed out from the courses.

===Chinese===
随着4月1日临近，我们怀着极大的期盼等候真正飞行的开始。与之相伴的，是等待考卷发榜的焦灼——任何两门不及格，就足以让卷主被课程淘汰。

===Original===
The flying instruction was carried on from two stages or different sections of the field. I was assigned to B stage which was about a half mile out in the field from the cadet barracks. Each instructor had about six cadets assigned to him, and early in the morning on the first day of April, our instruction commenced. I was assigned to Sergeant Winston, together with five other cadets. We pushed his instruction plane out from the hangar to the line. Sergeant Winston picked out one of us, told him to get into the rear cockpit and was off. The rest of us walked over to B stage, watching for tarantulas along the road on the way.

===Chinese===
飞行教学在两个阶段场地、即机场的两个不同分区进行。我被分到 B 阶段场地，位于学员营房外约半英里的场区。每位教官名下约有六名学员。4月1日一大早，教学就开始了。我和另外五名学员被分给温斯顿中士（Sergeant Winston）。我们把他那架教练机从机库推到停机线，温斯顿中士挑了我们中的一个，叫他坐进后座舱，随即起飞走了。我们其余人便步行前往 B 阶段场地，一路提防着路上的塔兰图拉毒蛛。

===Original===
In 1924, the Curtiss Jenny was still used by the Army for a training plane, although the 90 hp Curtiss OX-5 engines had been replaced by 150 hp Hispano-Suizas. The more modern types of planes for training were still in the experimental stage. The Jennies had been designed during the war and they were becoming obsolete, but it is doubtful whether a better training ship will ever be built, although undoubtedly the newest type is much safer. Jennies were underpowered; they were somewhat tricky and they splintered badly when they crashed hard; but when a cadet learned to fly one of them, well, he was just about capable of flying anything on wings with a reasonable degree of safety.

===Chinese===
1924年，陆军仍用柯蒂斯"珍妮"作教练机，只是90马力的柯蒂斯 OX-5 发动机已换成了150马力的伊斯帕诺-苏莎（Hispano-Suiza）——也就是"希索"。更现代的教练机型仍处于试验阶段。"珍妮"是大战期间设计的，渐显老态，但要说还能造出更好的教练机，倒也未必；尽管最新式机型无疑安全得多。"珍妮"动力不足，脾气有点怪，摔得狠了便散成碎木片；可一个学员一旦学会了飞"珍妮"，那么，凡有翅膀的东西，他都差不多能带着几分安全把握飞起来了。

===Original===
I had been particularly fortunate in my assignment of an instructor. Sergeant Winston held the record for flying time in the army with about thirty-three hundred hours. He was an excellent pilot and knew how to instruct if he wanted to. When my turn came he asked me how much flying time I had had and after I told him about three hundred and twenty-five hours he turned the controls over to me with orders to take the ship around and land it. I had some difficulty in flying with my right hand. The wartime ships which I was accustomed to were built to be flown with the left, but after the Armistice it was decided to change the throttle over to the other side on the theory that the right hand was the natural one to fly with. After three landings, however, Sergeant Winston got out of the cockpit and told me to fly around for thirty minutes and try to get used to right handed piloting.

===Chinese===
我在教官分配上格外走运。温斯顿中士保持着陆军飞行时间纪录，约三千三百小时。他是出色的飞行员，只要愿意，也懂得如何施教。轮到我时，他问我飞了多少小时，听我说约三百二十五小时后，便把操纵交到我手上，命令我绕场一圈然后降落。我用右手飞行有些别扭。我飞惯的战时飞机是按左手飞行设计的，但停战之后，军中认定右手才是飞行的天然用手，便把油门挪到了另一侧。不过三次降落之后，温斯顿中士就跳出座舱，叫我独自飞上三十分钟，试着习惯右手操纵。

===Original===
When we were not flying we were gathered around the stage house watching the progress of our classmates and learning how to turn the propellers over in starting the engine without placing ourselves in a position to be struck in case it kicked backwards. To a pilot, the propeller is the most dangerous part of his plane, and is a constant source of worry to him when his ship is on the ground among people who vie with each other in seeing how close they can stand to the whirring blade while the motor is still running. Then there is usually a contest to see who can be first to move it up and down after it stops turning over.

===Chinese===
不飞行时，我们就聚在阶段场房四周，观看同学们的进展，学习摇螺旋桨起动发动机时如何站位，以免发动机反转时挨揍。对飞行员来说，螺旋桨是飞机上最危险的部件：飞机停在地上时，人们总爱比赛谁能站得离呼呼转动的桨叶更近，这让他提心吊胆；等发动机停转，通常又有一场竞赛，看谁第一个凑上去把桨上下晃动。

===Original===
A cadet is usually given about ten hours of dual instruction before he is allowed to solo. The instructor first takes him up and after flying around for a few minutes, allows the student to take hold of the controls to get an approximate idea of the amount and direction of movement necessary for gentle banks and turns. Then the instructor throws his hands up in the air in full view of the student—the signal that he has turned over entire control of the ship. The cadet is given the opportunity suddenly to realize that flying is not a simple operation of pulling the stick back to go up and pushing it forward to come down, but that an instinctive and synchronized movement of all controls is necessary even to keep the machine in level flight.

===Chinese===
学员通常要接受约十小时的双座带飞才能获准单飞。教官先带他上天，飞几分钟后让学生握住操纵面，大致体会一下做柔和压坡度和转弯所需的动作量与方向。然后教官当着学生的面把双手高高举起——这是把飞机全部操纵权交出的信号。学员这才猛然认识到，飞行并非"带杆上升、推杆下降"的简单操作；哪怕只是让机器保持平飞，也需要全部操纵杆舵本能而协调的配合。

===Original===
For a moment after the pilot turns over the controls the plane keeps on a straight course, then the nose begins to lose its normal position on the horizon, a wing dips down, and a blast of air rushes in from one side of the cockpit. Carefully learned instructions are forgotten and the controls serve only to move the earth still farther from its proper position. All this time the instructor's hands are gripping the top of the cowling. The cadet realizes that it is up to himself in some manner to level the plane out into a normal flying position once more, not realizing for an instant that his instructor can operate the stick nearly as well with his knees as with his hand and that he has probably already saved the plane from falling into a spin several times.

===Chinese===
操纵权交出之后，飞机还能循直线飞一小会儿，接着机头便在地平线上渐渐偏离常态，一侧机翼往下沉，一股气流猛地从座舱一侧灌进来。精心背下的口诀忘得一干二净，杆舵一动，只会让大地离它本来的位置更远。而这整个过程中，教官的双手始终扣在整流罩顶沿上。学员明白，得靠他自己想方设法把飞机重新改平到正常飞行状态；他一刻也想不到，教官用膝盖操纵操纵杆几乎和用手一样灵光，而且多半已经好几次把飞机从坠入螺旋的边缘救了回来。

===Original===
After splashing around the sky in this manner for several minutes the pilot brings his ship back into position and pulling up into a stall with a throttled motor, roars back his instructions at a cadet who is much more absorbed in watching the approaching ground below than in listening to his instructor. When forty-five minutes have passed, the ship is flown back and landed near the stage house where the next cadet, with helmet and goggles adjusted, is waiting for his turn in the air. The first climbs out and takes his place on the bench surrounding the base of the building and the plane is off to repeat the performance over again.

===Chinese===
这样在天上扑腾几分钟后，教官把飞机恢复到位，收油门拉成失速姿态，冲着学员大声吼出他的训示——而那学员满脑子都是脚下步步逼近的地面，哪还听得进教官的话。四十五分钟一到，飞机便飞回场房附近降落，下一名学员已戴正头盔和风镜，等着轮到自己上天。前一个爬出座舱，在环绕场房墙根的长凳上落座，飞机又起飞去把这场戏重演一遍。

===Original===
At the end of ten hours, if the cadet is not capable of soloing he is in grave danger of being washed out as a flyer. However, if the instructor believes that a little more time will be sufficient and that the student has shown signs of eventually becoming a military pilot, the dual instruction may be continued for three or four more hours.

===Chinese===
十小时期满，学员若还不能单飞，就有被淘汰出飞行行列的极大危险。不过，教官若认为再稍加时日便足以奏效，且学员已显露出终能成为军航飞行员的迹象，双座带飞可再延长三四个小时。

===Original===
At Brooks when an instructor came to the conclusion that one of his students would never master the art of flying quickly enough to keep up with the standard of the class, he turned the cadet over for a check hop with the stage commander who was always a pilot of long experience. Few cadets ever passed this check; if the stage commander believed that any cadet had been misjudged, however, he had authority to place him back on flying status for further instruction. If the commander concurred with the decision of the instructor, he recommended the cadet for a final check on headquarters stage with the chief check pilot. The decision of this officer was final and to be returned to flying after a flight with him was an occurrence seldom recorded in cadet history. After failing his final check flight a cadet was ordered to appear before a board of officers known as the "Benzine Board." If he was reporting for misconduct or academic deficiency there was still some slight hope of beating the board, but if it was for inability to fly, the decision of "washout" was a foregone conclusion.

===Chinese===
在布鲁克斯，教官一旦断定某个学员永远无法足够快地掌握飞行技艺、跟上全班的进度，便把该学员移交阶段指挥官做考核飞行——阶段指挥官向来由资深飞行员担任。能通过这种考核的学员寥寥无几；但阶段指挥官若认为某个学员受了错判，有权让他恢复飞行身份继续受训。若指挥官认同教官的判断，便将该学员上报到总部阶段场地，由首席考核飞行员做最终考核。这位军官的裁定即是终审；跟他飞过一遭还能重返蓝天的，在学员史上几乎查无记载。最终考核飞行失利后，学员即奉命到军官委员会——即"汽油委员会"——面前报到。若是因行为不端或学业不佳而去，或许还有一线生机；若因不会飞行而去，"淘汰"的裁定便是板上钉钉。

===Original===
The washing out for our class commenced in earnest with the approach of solo flights and the returns from our examinations. I was fortunate enough to have passed them and my previous flying experience kept me from worrying on any other account during the first part of our training.

===Chinese===
随着单飞临近、考卷发榜，我们期的淘汰动起了真格。我侥幸门门通过，而此前的飞行经历又让我在训练头一阶段里无须为任何旁的事情操心。

===Original===
There was no disgrace in washing out. It simply meant in the majority of cases, that the cadet was not especially adapted to flying and he was sent back to his point of enlistment with an honorable discharge and the advice to take up some other form of occupation.

===Chinese===
淘汰并不是什么丢脸的事。这只意味着，就多数情形而言，该学员不太适合飞行；他会领着光荣退伍证被送回入伍地点，外加一句改行的忠告。

===Original===
Our first "Benzine Board" met about a month after the start of school and reconvened more or less regularly from that time until we were ready to be graduated from the primary school and transferred to Kelly for instructions on service types of planes.

===Chinese===
我们的第一届"汽油委员会"在开学约一个月后开庭，此后或多或少定期复会，直到我们即将从初级学校毕业、转往凯利学习现役机型为止。

===Original===
With the washing out process our barracks became less congested. It was not unusual to see the fellows on both sides pack up and cheerfully depart for destinations in different corners of the United States. After a few weeks there would be one bunk standing where eight had been—this in some part of the barracks on which the decisions of the "Benzine Board" had fallen hardest. In another case an entire bay was washed out and left entirely vacant. We never knew who would be next to go, and we could only continue to plug along as best we could with our flying and study a little harder on our ground-school work while we waited for the almost weekly list of washouts to be published on our bulletin board. We were in the full swing of cadet life and under the constant apprehension which accompanies it.

===Chinese===
随着淘汰的推进，营房不再拥挤。眼看左右铺的伙伴打好行囊、兴高采烈地奔赴合众国四面八方，已不是稀罕事。几个星期后，原先摆八张床的地方只剩一张——这是营房里"汽油委员会"裁决落得最狠的地段；还有一回，整整一个隔间的学员全体淘汰，留下一片空铺。我们永远不知道下一个走的是谁，只能照旧在飞行上埋头苦撑，地面课程的功课再加把劲，等着几乎每周一期张榜公布的淘汰名单。我们正处在学员生活的全盛节奏之中，也时时笼罩在与之相伴的忧惧之下。

===Original===
Along with our trials and worries went the fascination of flying together with the priceless goal before us of graduation with an Air Service commission. The wings of the service would be for those of us who were able to survive the rigid training and discipline of a year in the United States Army flying schools.

===Chinese===
与磨难和忧惧同行的，是飞行的魅力，以及悬在我们前方那个无价的目标——毕业时获得航空队军官委任。军中翼章将属于我们之中那些熬过美国陆军飞行学校一年严苛训练与纪律的人。

===Original===
Always there was something new to look forward to. The start of actual flying; the first solo; learning various stunts and maneuvers; transitions from Jennies to faster and quicker ships; and finally our transfer to Kelly Field, the alma mater of Army fliers.

===Chinese===
总有新的盼头：真正飞行的开始、首次单飞、学习种种特技与机动、从"珍妮"换飞更快更灵的飞机，以及最后转场到凯利机场——陆军飞行员的摇篮。

===Original===
The Army Air Service was an exacting instructor. There was no favoritism shown and no amount of politics could keep a cadet from being washed out if he fell down in flying. As a result, only a small percent of those entering Brooks ever graduate from Kelly. In our class of one hundred and four, thirty-three finished their primary training and only eighteen of us received our wings. This appears on the surface to be an unusually low number but as a result of the rigid requirements and careful instruction, our Air Corps schools rank among the best in the world today. They have an extremely low fatality list, not one man in our class being seriously injured.

===Chinese===
陆军航空队是位严苛的教员。它不讲情面，任凭你在政界多有门路，只要飞行上垮了台，谁也保不住你不被淘汰。其结果是，进布鲁克斯的人里只有一小部分能从凯利毕业。我们那期一百零四人，读完初级训练的三十三人，最后领到翼章的只有我们十八个。表面看来这个数目低得出奇，但正是靠着严苛的要求与精心的施教，我们的航空兵团学校才跻身当今世界一流；它们的死亡名单极短，我们那期没有一人受重伤。

===Original===
Probably the most exciting period in our flying training was when the soloing began. The instructor would climb out of his cockpit, tie a white handkerchief on the rudder as a danger signal, indicating that the ship was usually out of control, and signal his student to take off. In some cases the plane would take off nicely, circle the field and make a comparatively good landing. In others the landing would amount to a series of bounces, resulting in the necessity of a second or third attempt before the wheels would hold contact with the ground more than a fraction of a second at a time.
